### ✅ Pattern Implemented: よりほかない (have no choice but / nothing but)

**JLPT Level**: N2
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 4 (moderate priority - detected and highlighted)

**Pattern**: より他（ほか）ない is a structure that emphasizes that only (A) is possible, when attached to the end of verbs. This grammar pattern is a combination of the case marking particle より 'than' or 'from', the noun 他（ほか） 'other', and the い-Adjective ない. From this it can be translated directly as 'there is nothing other than (A)'. Common interpretations include 'to have no choice but to (A)', 'there is no other way than (A)', or simply 'have to (A)'.

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering all structure variants
1. Verb + より + ほか + ない: 靴に穴が空いたので、新しいのを買うよりほかない (15-23) - "I have no choice but to buy new shoes"
2. Verb + より + ほか + は + ない: 私が助けるよりほかはなかった (22-34) - "I had no choice but to help her"
3. Verb + より + ほか + に + ない: 被害者が無事であることを祈るよりほかにない (12-21) - "There is nothing we can do other than pray"
4. Verb + より + ほか + には + ない: 新しい仕事を探すよりほかにはない (17-27) - "I have no choice but to look for a new job"

**Implementation**: Matcher in n2.rs (lines 291-358), tests in n2_patterns.rs (lines 2626-2676)

**Key notes**:
- Pattern structure: Verb[基本形] + より + ほか + (は/に/には) + ない
- より tokenizes as 助詞/格助詞/一般 (case-marking particle)
- ほか tokenizes as 名詞/副詞可能 (noun with adverbial capability)
- Optional particles は (係助詞) and/or に (格助詞) can appear between ほか and ない
- ない can be 形容詞 (adjective) or 助動詞 (auxiliary verb)
- Handles both present (ない) and past (なかった) forms

**Tokenization observations**:
- より: surface="より", base="より", pos=助詞/格助詞/一般
- ほか: surface="ほか", base="ほか", pos=名詞/副詞可能
- は: surface="は", pos=助詞/係助詞
- に: surface="に", pos=助詞/格助詞
- ない: base="ない", pos=形容詞/自立 OR 助動詞

**Pattern ranges**: Include full construction from verb through final ない
- Basic form: 買うよりほかない (verb + より + ほか + ない)
- With particles: 助けるよりほかはなかった, 祈るよりほかにない, 探すよりほかにはない
- Past forms automatically handled by NaiMatcher checking base_form="ない"

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: に相違ない (without a doubt, no mistaking)

**JLPT Level**: N2
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 4 (moderate priority - detected and highlighted)

**Pattern**: に相違（そうい）ない is a structure that firmly states that something is 'without a doubt (A)', or that there is 'no mistaking (A)'. Similar to に違いない, it may be used to strengthen one's own opinions or to strongly agree with the opinion of another. Although 相違 may be used by itself meaning 'to differ', 相違ない is considered as a set い-Adjective. The literal meaning is 'there is no discrepancy in (A)'. This grammar structure is very formal, with に違いない almost always being used in its place.

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all structure variants
1. Verb + に相違ない: いい大学に行けるに相違ない (19-27) - "There's no doubt that he can go to a good university"
2. い-Adjective + に相違ない: 美味しいに相違ない (29-38) - "There is no doubt that it will be delicious"
3. な-Adjective + に相違ない: 便利に相違ない (17-24) - "There is no doubt that it is convenient"
4. Noun + に相違ない: 人に相違ない (10-16) - "There is no doubt that that person is the culprit"
5. から + に相違ない: からに相違ない (22-29) - "There is no doubt that he's being restless because he's lying"

**Implementation**: Matcher in n2.rs (lines 424-466), tests in n2_patterns.rs (lines 2689-2751)

**Key notes**:
- Pattern structure: Verb/Adj/Noun/(から) + に + 相違 + ない
- **IMPORTANT**: Must use kanji 相違, NOT hiragana そうい (different tokenization)
- に can be 助詞/格助詞/一般 OR 助詞/副詞化 (when after から)
- 相違 tokenizes as 名詞/サ変接続
- ない tokenizes as 形容詞/自立 (adjective form)

**Tokenization observations**:
- に (before から): surface="に", pos=助詞/格助詞/一般
- に (after から): surface="に", pos=助詞/副詞化
- 相違: surface="相違", base="相違", pos=名詞/サ変接続
- ない: base="ない", pos=形容詞/自立
- Hiragana "にそういない" tokenizes as: に + そう (副詞) + い (いる verb) + ない (auxiliary) ❌

**Pattern ranges**: Include preceding element + に相違ない
- Verb form: 行けるに相違ない (verb + に相違ない)
- Adjective form: 美味しいに相違ない, 便利に相違ない
- Noun form: 人に相違ない (noun + に相違ない)
- から emphasis: からに相違ない (から + に相違ない)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: どうやら (apparently, it seems like)

**JLPT Level**: N2
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 4 (moderate priority - detected and highlighted)

**Pattern**: どうやら is an adverb that can be translated as 'apparently' or 'it seems like'. It is often grouped together with other phrases of speculation such as ようだ, そうだ, and らしい. どうやら strengthens the uncertainty of things the speaker is already unsure about, similar to "in some way it seems like (A)".

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering different usage contexts
1. どうやら + みたいだ: 鈴木くんはいるか？どうやら今日も休んでいるみたいだね (9-13) - "It seems like he's taking today off as well"
2. どうやら + ようだ: どうやら私は彼女には必要がないようだ (0-4) - "It seems like I am not needed by her"
3. どうやら + らしい: どうやら彼は結婚しているらしいよ (0-4) - "He is apparently married"
4. Mid-sentence usage: お隣の鈴木さんがどうやら来月からイギリスに行くらしいですよ (8-12) - "Our neighbor is apparently going to England"

**Implementation**: Matcher in n2.rs (lines 253-270), tests in n2_patterns.rs (lines 2355-2421)

**Key notes**:
- どうやら tokenizes as 副詞/一般 (general adverb)
- Simple single-token pattern matching surface='どうやら' and base_form='どうやら'
- Can appear at the beginning of sentences or mid-sentence
- Often used with speculation markers: みたいだ, ようだ, そうだ, らしい
- Etymology: Combination of どう 'how' and やら (particle denoting uncertainty)

**Tokenization observations**:
- surface="どうやら", base_form="どうやら", pos=副詞/一般
- Consistent tokenization as a single adverb token
- No conjugation forms (static adverb)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: なにやら (something or other, some kind of, for some reason)

**JLPT Level**: N2
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 4 (moderate priority - detected and highlighted)

**Pattern**: 何（なに）やら is an adverb expressing uncertainty about what exactly something is or why something is happening. Similar to どうやら, it is composed of the question word 何（なに） and the particle やら which denotes uncertainty and self-questioning. Different from 何か which can be used for general statements, 何（なに）やら is only used when the speaker is unsure of what the thing in question is.

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering different translation contexts
1. "something": あの人に何やら言われても何にも感じない (4-7) - "I don't feel anything when that person says something to me"
2. "some kind of": やっと子供が落ち着いたと思ったら、何やら歌い出した (17-20) - "He started to sing some kind of song"
3. "for some reason": 家に帰ってカバンを開けたら何やら見覚えのないレシートが入ってた (13-16) - "There was a receipt in there for some reason"
4. "something happened": あの二人の間で何やらあったらしい (7-10) - "Apparently something happened between those two"

**Implementation**: Matcher in n2.rs (lines 272-289), tests in n2_patterns.rs (lines 2423-2486)

**Key notes**:
- なにやら tokenizes as 副詞/一般 (general adverb), same as どうやら
- Simple single-token pattern matching surface='何やら' and base_form='何やら'
- Expresses uncertainty about what something is (not general "something" like 何か)
- Translation depends on context: "something or other", "some kind of", "for some reason"
- Etymology: 何（なに） + やら (particle denoting uncertainty)

**Tokenization observations**:
- surface="何やら", base_form="何やら", pos=副詞/一般
- Consistent tokenization as a single adverb token
- No conjugation forms (static adverb)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: ～ざる (attributive form of classical negative auxiliary ず)

**JLPT Level**: N2
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 4 (moderate priority - detected and highlighted)

**Pattern**: ざる is the attributive form of the classical auxiliary verb ず. It keeps the same negation 'not' meaning as ず, and conjugates with verbs in the same way as ない. ざる is often translated as "un~" (like "unstoppable", "unknown") and is primarily used in set expressions or before nouns (attributive position).

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering various verb types
1. 五段 verb: 言わざる人 (13-17) - "a person who will not complain"
2. Passive construction: 知られざる過去 (20-23, れざる) - "the unknown past"
3. 一段 verb: 与えざる影響 (10-14) - "influence that cannot be given"
4. 一段 verb: 恐れざる勇気 (6-10) - "fearless courage"

**Implementation**: Matcher in n2.rs (lines 163-184), tests in n2_patterns.rs (lines 2050-2119)

**Key notes**:
- Classical auxiliary ざる has base_form="ぬ" and conjugation form "体言接続" (attributive)
- Attaches to verbs in 未然形 (irrealis/negative form)
- For passive constructions like 知られざる, ざる attaches to the auxiliary れる (in 未然形)
- Primarily used in formal/literary contexts and set expressions
- Modern Japanese prefers ない for most negations

**Tokenization observations**:
- surface="ざる", base_form="ぬ", pos=助動詞
- features[4]="特殊・ヌ", features[5]="体言接続"
- Appears after verbs/auxiliaries in 未然形

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: つもりで (with the intention of / as if / pretend)

**JLPT Level**: N2
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 4 (moderate priority - detected and highlighted)

**Pattern**: つもりで is a combination of the noun つもり 'intention' and the case marking particle で 'with'. This structure conveys different meanings depending on what comes before it:
- With verbs/adjectives/nouns: "with the intention of (A)", "believing that (A)"
- With past-tense verbs: "as if (A)", "pretending that (A)", "acting like (A)"

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all major structure variants
1. Verb + つもりで: 買わないつもりで (4-10) - "without any intention of buying"
2. Verb[た] + つもりで: なったつもりで (10-15) - "as if you were" (past = pretend/act as if)
3. Noun + の + つもりで: 冗談のつもりで (2-7) - "with the intention of making a joke"
4. な-Adjective + な + つもりで: 真剣なつもりで (2-7) - "with serious intention"
5. Verb[た] + つもりで: 選んだつもりで (7-12) - "believing that I chose"

**Implementation**: Matcher in n2.rs (lines 186-219), tests in n2_patterns.rs (lines 2121-2200)

**Key notes**:
- つもり always tokenizes as 名詞/非自立/一般 (non-independent noun)
- で is a 助詞/格助詞 (case-marking particle)
- Pattern uses Optional(Any) + つもり + で to capture preceding element
- Past-tense verbs give a different meaning: "as if" rather than "with the intention of"
- Range includes the preceding element (verb/particle/auxiliary) + つもりで

**Tokenization observations**:
- surface="つもり", base_form="つもり", pos=名詞/非自立/一般
- surface="で", base_form="で", pos=助詞/格助詞/一般
- Preceded by verbs (any form), の (particle), な (auxiliary), た/だ (auxiliary)

## Session 378 - 2025-12-14

### ✅ Session Summary
**Status**: COMPLETE - Successfully implemented 得る・得る pattern (N2)
**Patterns completed**: 1 (得る・得る - N2 newly implemented)
**Tests added**: 6 new tests (all passing)
**Total tests**: 2435 passing, 1 ignored (was 2429 passing, 1 ignored - net +6 tests)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: 得る・得る (to be possible, can do)

**JLPT Level**: N2
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 4 (moderate priority - detected and highlighted)

**Pattern**: 得（え）る is a る-Verb that has the meaning of 'to gain', or 'to acquire'. When attached to the conjunctive form (連用形) of verbs, it takes on the nuance of 'for (A) to be possible', or 'to be able to (A)'. This is more formal/bookish than できる and conveys a sense of possibility or potential.

**Key feature**: Can be written as either える or うる. The うる form is more literary and is often preferred when the preceding verb stem ends in え (e.g., 考えうる rather than 考ええる).

**Structures tested**: 6 comprehensive tests covering all major conjugation forms
1. Verb[stem] + える (dictionary): 死にえるだろう → 死にえるだろう (10-17)
2. Verb[stem] + える (dictionary): 起こりえるものだ → 起こりえる (17-22)
3. Verb[stem] + える (dictionary): なりえるので → なりえる (12-16)
4. Verb[stem] + うる (literary form): 考えうる事は → 考えうる (0-4)
5. Verb[stem] + えます (polite): ありえますね → ありえます (3-8)
6. Verb[stem] + えない (negative): ありえない → ありえない (20-25)

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n2.rs (eru_u30fb_eru function, lines 5-33)
  - VerbStemMatcher: Checks pos=動詞 AND conjugation=連用形
  - EruUruMatcher: Matches える OR うる with pos=動詞/非自立
  - Pattern: Verb[連用形] + える/うる (動詞/非自立)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n2_patterns.rs (eru_u30fb_eru_tests module, lines 1902-1978)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (declared with priority 4)

**Tokenization observations**:
- Split tokenization (productive): Verb stem + える/うる as separate tokens
  - 起こり (起こる, 連用形) + える (動詞/非自立, 基本形)
  - 考え (考える, 連用形) + うる (動詞/非自立, 基本形)
  - え/うる conjugates: 基本形 (える), 連用形 (え + ます), 未然形 (え + ない)
- Lexicalized compounds (not matched): ありえる as single token (動詞/自立)
  - Our pattern focuses on the productive construction, not dictionary verbs

**Pattern ranges**: Include verb stem + える/うる + any conjugation auxiliaries
- Dictionary form: `起こりえる` (17-22) - verb stem + える
- Polite form: `ありえます` (3-8) - verb stem + え + ます
- Negative form: `ありえない` (20-25) - verb stem + え + ない
- Volitional: `死にえるだろう` (10-17) - verb stem + える + だろう

**Linguistic notes**:
- える vs うる: Both are valid. うる is more literary and preferred when the verb stem ends in え
- Common phrases: ありえる (possible), ありえない (impossible) are very common in daily speech
- Formal register: More bookish than できる, used for abstract possibilities
- Compared to できる: える emphasizes that "the possibility has been gained/acquired"

**Related patterns**:
- 〜得ない (u301c_enai) - N2, already implemented (negative potential form)
- できる - N5 (general ability/possibility, more casual)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: 代わりに (in exchange for / instead of)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 3 (low priority - detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: 代（か）わり "a substitute" is a noun that is used to convey when (B) is acting as a replacement or substitute for (A). The case marking particle に comes after 代わり.

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering all structure variants
1. Verb + 代わりに (電話する代わりに - in exchange for calling): 俺が社長に電話する代わりに、お前はお客さんに電話をしておいて。 → 電話する代わりに (5-13)
2. [い]Adjective + 代わりに (古い代わりに - to substitute for being old): あのプールは古い代わりに安い。 → 古い代わりに (6-12)
3. [な]Adjective + な + 代わりに (大変な代わりに - in exchange for being difficult): 仕事は大変な代わりに楽しい。 → な代わりに (5-10)
4. Noun + の + 代わりに (私の代わりに - in my place): 私の代わりにやってください。 → の代わりに (1-6)

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (kawarini function, lines 9551-9584)
  - Custom matcher for 代わり/替わり as noun (名詞/一般)
  - Custom matcher for に as case particle (助詞/格助詞)
  - Pattern: Optional(Any) + 代わり + に
  - Allows optional preceding element (verb, adjective, noun + の, or nothing)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (kawarini_tests module, lines 13881-13937)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (declared as Kawarini variant)

**Tokenization observations**:
- 代わり always tokenizes as 名詞/一般
- に always tokenizes as 助詞/格助詞/一般
- Preceding element varies: verb (基本形), い-Adj (基本形), な-Adj (名詞/形容動詞語幹 + な), or noun + の
- Pattern ranges include preceding word + 代わりに for full grammatical context
- Both 代わり and 替わり kanji variants are supported

**Note**: Standalone 代わりに usage at sentence start (meaning "instead" or "rather than that") is an idiomatic expression not covered by the structural pattern tests.

## Session 374 - 2025-12-14

### ✅ Session Summary
**Status**: COMPLETE - Successfully implemented 込む ① and 込む ② patterns (N3)
**Patterns completed**: 2 (込む ① and 込む ② - N3 newly implemented)
**Tests added**: 10 new tests (all passing)
**Total tests**: 2405 passing, 1 ignored (was 2395 passing, 1 ignored - net +10 tests)

### ✅ Patterns Implemented: 込む ① and 込む ② (to put into / to do deeply)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority - detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: 込む is a verb suffix that attaches to the conjunctive form of other verbs. It has two primary meanings that tokenize identically:
- 込む ①: To enter completely into doing (A), to put into (A), to go into (A)
- 込む ②: To remain in (A), to thoroughly do (A), to do (A) deeply/intensely

**Key Discovery**: Initially tried matching hiragana "こむ" but compound verbs use kanji "込む" in their base_form (e.g., 押し込む, 飲み込む, 座り込む). The matcher checks `token.base_form.ends_with("込む")` NOT `ends_with("こむ")`.

**Structures tested**: 10 comprehensive tests covering both patterns and both structure variants

**込む ① tests** (5 tests):
1. Verb[stem] + 込む (押し込む - push into): ピンを壁に押し込むのは難しい。 → 押し込む (5-9)
2. Verb[stem] + 込む (飲み込む - swallow): 薬を飲み込むのが苦手です。 → 飲み込む (2-6)
3. Verb[stem] + 込んだ (走り込んだ - ran into): ドアが閉まる前に電車に走り込んだ。 → 走り込んだ (11-16)
4. Verb[stem] + 込む (飛び込む - leap into): サウナから出て水風呂に飛び込んだ。 → 飛び込んだ (11-16)
5. Verb[stem] + 込みます (polite): 毎朝このジュースを飲み込みます。 → 飲み込みます (9-15)

**込む ② tests** (5 tests):
1. Verb[stem] + 込む (座り込む - remain seated): 道の真ん中で座り込んでいる人がいた。 → 座り込ん (6-10)
2. Verb[stem] + 込む (話し込む - get lost in talking): もうこんな時間か、話し込んじゃったな。 → 話し込ん (9-13)
3. Verb[stem] + 込む negative (考え込まない - don't think deeply): そんなに考え込まなくてもいいよ。 → 考え込まなく (4-10)
4. Verb[stem] + 込む (思い込む - jump to conclusions): 彼女は自分が天才だと思い込んでいる。 → 思い込ん (10-14)
5. Verb[stem] + 込みます (polite): この問題について深く考え込みます。 → 考え込みます (10-16)

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (komu_u2460 and komu_u2461 functions, lines 8825-8867)
  - Custom matcher for compound verbs ending in 込む (kanji, NOT hiragana)
  - Checks: pos="動詞" AND base_form.ends_with("込む") AND base_form != "込む"
  - Both patterns use identical matchers (semantically different but structurally identical)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (komu_u2460_tests and komu_u2461_tests modules, lines 13691-13823)
- Patterns: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (KomuU2460 and KomuU2461 variants, lines 4273-4289)

**Tokenization observations**:
- Compound verbs tokenize as single tokens: 押し込む, 飲み込む, 座り込む, etc.
- Base form always uses kanji 込 not hiragana こ: base_form="押し込む" (not "押しこむ")
- All conjugations work: 込む (dictionary), 込み (stem), 込ん (past), 込ま (negative)
- Pattern ranges extend with auxiliary verbs: 飲み込みます includes ます

**Note on ambiguity**: Like らしい① vs らしい②, both 込む patterns tokenize identically. When detected, the application should show both grammar explanations and let the user determine meaning from context.

## Session 373 - 2025-12-14

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: 切れない (unable to finish completely / can't possibly)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 3 (low priority - detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: In the negative-potential form, 切（き）れない will attach to the conjunctive form of other verbs, to express that a particular action is 'unable to be completed'. Unlike 切る (which shows something was done to the point of running out), 切れない often implies that the verb cannot be completed specifically because there is too much/many of something in order to finish it. It may also imply that something cannot be done 'to the point of satisfaction'.

**Structures tested**: 6 comprehensive tests covering both structure variants
1. Verb[stem] + 切れない (can't finish eating): 大盛頼むの？いくらあなたでもここの大盛は食べきれないと思うよ。 → 食べきれない (20-26)
2. Verb[stem] + 切れない (can't finish climbing): あの山は一日では登りきれない。 → 登りきれない (8-14)
3. Verb[stem] + 切れない (can't finish counting): 宇宙には数えきれないほどの星がある。 → 数えきれない (4-10)
4. Verb[stem] + 切れない (can't wait): 楽しみ過ぎて待ちきれない。 → 待ちきれない (6-12)
5. Verb[stem] + 切れない (can't express): 私のこの気持ちは言葉では表しきれない。 → 表しきれない (12-18)
6. Verb[stem] + 切れません (polite): この料理は多すぎて全部食べきれません。 → 食べきれません (11-18)

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (kirenai function, lines 8591-8613)
  - Custom matcher for きれる as auxiliary verb (negative potential form)
  - Must be 一段 (ichidan) conjugation, pos=動詞/非自立
  - Distinguishes from きる (五段・ラ行 godan-ra conjugation)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (kirenai_tests module, lines 13615-13689)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Kirenai variant, lines 4201-4207)

**Tokenization observations**:
- Standard form: Verb[連用形] + きれ[未然形] + ない
- Polite form: Verb[連用形] + きれ[連用形] + ませ + ん
- きれる has base_form="きれる", pos=動詞/非自立, features[4]="一段"
- Pattern ranges include the full construction (verb stem + きれない/きれません)

## Session 371 - 2025-12-14 (Updated)

### ✅ Session Summary
**Status**: COMPLETE - Successfully implemented 2 N3 patterns (必ずしも, 向け)
**Patterns completed**: 2 (必ずしも, 向け - both N3 newly implemented)
**Tests added**: 12 new tests total (8 for 必ずしも + 4 for 向け, all passing)
**Total tests**: 2378 passing, 1 ignored (was 2366 passing, 1 ignored - net +12 tests)

### ✅ Pattern 1 Implemented: 必ずしも (not necessarily/not always)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority - detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: Although it is often considered a stand-alone adverb, 必（かなら）ずしも is a combination of the adverb 必（かなら）ず 'invariably' or 'certainly', and the adverbial particles し and も. The particles し and も are combined to strengthen the meaning of the word before it. Due to this, 必ずしも may be translated as "absolutely certainly". However, 必ずしも is an expression that is only used in negative sentences, so is commonly translated as 'not always (A)', 'not entirely (A)', or 'not necessarily (A)'. The literal meaning is close to 'not absolutely certainly (A)'. This pattern is often used at the beginning of a phrase, before expressing something that is not 100% certain.

**Structures tested**: 8 comprehensive tests covering all structure variants
1. 必ずしも + Phrase + わけではない: 男の人は必ずしも強いわけではない → 必ずしも (4-8)
2. 必ずしも + Phrase + わけじゃない: お金持ちが必ずしも幸せなわけじゃないよ → 必ずしも (5-9)
3. 必ずしも + Phrase + とは限らない: 刑務所にいる人たちは必ずしも犯罪を犯したとは限らない → 必ずしも (10-14)
4. 必ずしも + Phrase + とは言えない: 女の人は必ずしも買い物が好きだとは言えない → 必ずしも (4-8)
5. 必ずしも + Phrase + わけではありません: 高価な物が必ずしも良いわけではありません → 必ずしも (5-9)
6. 必ずしも + Phrase + わけじゃありません: 新しいものが必ずしも正しいわけじゃありません → 必ずしも (6-10)
7. 必ずしも + Phrase + とは限りません: 有名な医者が必ずしも腕が良いとは限りません → 必ずしも (6-10)
8. 必ずしも + Phrase + とは言えません: 学歴が高い人が必ずしも成功するとは言えません → 必ずしも (7-11)

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (kanarazushimo function, lines 8487-8500)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (kanarazushimo_tests module, lines 13324-13414)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Kanarazushimo variant, lines 4153-4159)

### ✅ Pattern 2 Implemented: 向け (intended for/aimed at)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority - detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: 向（む）け is a shortened form of the transitive verb 向（む）ける 'to aim at', and is considered to be a 造語（ぞうご） (neologism). This means a word that was created for a specific purpose, and may not necessarily be used for anything outside of that purpose. The purpose of 向（む）け is to attach to other nouns, in order to express that something is 'intended for (A)', 'aimed at (A)', or 'oriented toward (A)'. 向け may be seen followed by the case marking particle に (when followed by a phrase), or the case marking particle の, when linked with another noun.

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering both structure variants
1. Noun + 向け + に: この教科書は上級者向けに書いてある → 者向け (8-11)
2. Noun + 向け + に: 子供向けに書かれた本を読んで日本語を勉強したら？ → 子供向け (0-4)
3. Noun + 向け + の + Noun: オフィス向けのパソコンを１０台買うとしたらいくらになりますか → オフィス向け (0-6)
4. Noun + 向け + の + Noun: これは大人向けの映画だから、お前には見せれないよ → 大人向け (3-7)

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (muke function, lines 8527-8544)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (muke_tests module, lines 13424-13470)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Muke variant, lines 4177-4183)

**Tokenization pattern**:
```
向け suffix:
- surface='向け', base='向け'
- POS: 名詞/接尾/一般 (suffix noun)
- Always follows a noun
- Can be followed by に (purpose/direction) or の (modifier)

Examples:
- 上級者向けに (for advanced learners)
- 子供向けに (for children)
- オフィス向けの (intended for office use)
- 大人向けの (for adults)
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Two-token matcher: Noun + MukeMatcher
2. MukeMatcher checks surface='向け', base='向け', POS=名詞/接尾
3. Pattern priority: 1 (low - construction pattern, detected but not highlighted)

**Pattern ranges**: Include Noun + 向け
- Captures the full construction: preceding noun + 向け
- Does NOT include following particles (に, の)
- Examples: 子供向け (0-4), 大人向け (3-7), オフィス向け (0-6)

**Linguistic Note**: 向け comes from the transitive verb 向ける 'to aim at' and is used to express that something has been specifically designed or intended for a particular purpose or audience. This differs from the related suffix 向き (from intransitive 向く) which expresses something that faces toward (A), but perhaps not intentionally. 向け implies intentional design, while 向き implies suitability.

**Related patterns**:
- 向き (N3) - "suitable for / facing toward" (not yet implemented - similar but distinct pattern)

- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (kanarazushimo function, lines 8487-8500)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (kanarazushimo_tests module, lines 13324-13414)

```
必ずしも adverb:
- surface='必ずしも', base='必ずしも'
- POS: 副詞/一般 (general adverb)
- Always a single token
- Appears at the beginning of phrases
- Always used with negative constructions (わけではない, とは限らない, とは言えない)
```

**Key implementation points**:

**Test Quality**:

- Cover both polite forms (わけではありません, とは限りません, とは言えません)
- Include variations (わけじゃない, わけじゃありません)
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: 必ずしも is unique in that it's only used in negative sentences. The combination of し and も particles strengthens the base adverb 必ず (invariably/certainly), but the pattern's use in negative contexts creates the meaning "not absolutely/not necessarily". This is a grammaticalized construction where the individual components (必ず + しも) have fused into a single adverbial unit that collocates specifically with negative predicates.

**Usage contexts**:
- Expressing exceptions to general rules: "Men aren't necessarily strong"
- Challenging assumptions: "Expensive things aren't always good"
- Nuanced negation: Softens absolute negatives into partial negatives
- Formal and informal registers (polite and casual forms tested)
- Always precedes a negative predicate (わけではない, とは限らない, とは言えない)

**Related patterns**:

## Session 367 - 2025-12-14

### ✅ Session Summary
**Status**: COMPLETE - Successfully implemented 1 N3 pattern (当たり)
**Patterns completed**: 1 (当たり - N3 newly implemented)
**Tests added**: 4 new tests for 当たり (all passing)
**Total tests**: 2344 passing, 1 ignored (was 2340 passing, 1 ignored - net +4 tests)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: 当たり (per / each)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority - detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: When attached to the end of numbers or counters, the noun 当（あ）たり 'to hit' will be used as a suffix. In these cases, the meaning is usually translated as 'per (A)', or 'each (A)'. The pattern expresses a relationship similar to に対（たい）して 'to be in relation to', where (A) 当たり (B) means "Regarding (A), (B) equates to it."

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering both forms
1. Counter + あたり (hiragana): 一個あたり → あたり (2-5)
2. Counter + あたり + の: 一日あたりの → あたり (2-5)
3. Counter + 当たり (kanji): １台当たり → 当たり (6-9)
4. Counter + 当たり: 一人当たり → 当たり (2-5)

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (atari function, lines 6760-6779)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (atari_tests module, lines 12966-13012)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Atari variant, lines 3849-3855)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
当たり/あたり suffix:
Hiragana form "あたり":
- surface='あたり', base='あたり'
- POS: 名詞/接尾/一般 (suffix noun)

Kanji form "当たり":
- surface='当たり', base='当たり'
- POS: 名詞/一般 (general noun)

Both follow number + counter constructions:
- 一個あたり (one item per)
- １台当たり (per vehicle)
- 一人当たり (per person)
- 一日あたり (per day)
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Single-token matcher: AtariMatcher
2. Matches both surface forms: "あたり" OR "当たり"
3. Matches both base forms: "あたり" OR "当たり"
4. Accepts two POS patterns: 名詞/接尾 (suffix) or 名詞/一般 (general noun)
5. Pattern priority: 1 (low - construction pattern, detected but not highlighted)

**Pattern ranges**: Include only あたり/当たり (3 characters)
- Hiragana: `あたり` (chars 2-5) - the suffix itself
- Kanji: `当たり` (chars 6-9 or 2-5) - the suffix itself
- Pattern captures just the suffix marker after the counter

**Test Quality**:
- All 4 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover both hiragana and kanji forms, with and without following の
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: While the literal meaning of 当（あ）たり is 'to meet' (as in 'to come into physical contact with'), as a suffix it has a nuance similar to に対（たい）して 'to be in relation to'. The pattern expresses equivalence or ratio: "(A) 当たり (B)" means "Regarding (A), (B) equates to it" or "per (A), there is (B)".

**Usage contexts**:
- Rates and ratios: Price per item, calories per serving
- Distributions: Per person, per day, per unit
- Formal/informal: Works in both registers
- Always follows number + counter construction

**Related patterns**:
- に当たる (N3) - "corresponds to / amounts to" (different meaning, already implemented)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: 合う (to do mutually/reciprocally)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority - detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: The う-Verb 合（あ）う when following the conjunctive form (連用形/stem) of another verb means to do that action in concert with another person/group. Translated as "to mutually do (A)", "to do (A) for one another", or "to do (A) reciprocally". The key meaning is that involvement from both sides is required - the action is performed together or toward each other.

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering major conjugations
1. Verb[stem] + 合う (dictionary): 話しあう → 話しあう (8-12)
2. Verb[stem] + 合う (present): 見せあう → 見せあう (11-15)
3. Verb[stem] + 合った (past): 見せあった → 見せあった (11-16)
4. Verb[stem] + 合う (with nominalizer): 助けあう → 助けあう (8-12)
5. Verb[stem] + 合わない (negative): 協力しあわない → 協力しあわない (18-25)

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (au function, lines 2763-2793)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (au_tests module, lines 12670-12736)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Au variant, lines 3112-3118)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
合う suffix (split tokenization):
- Verb[stem] (動詞/自立, 連用形) + あう (動詞/非自立)
- Base form: あう OR ある (both appear in Kagome)
- Surface: あう, あっ, あわ, etc. (various conjugations)
- POS: 動詞/非自立 (dependent verb)

Common verbs with split tokenization:
- 話しあう (話し + あう) - talk together
- 見せあう (見せ + あう) - show each other
- 助けあう (助け + あう) - help each other
- 協力しあう (協力し + あう) - cooperate together

Note: Some combinations like 取りあう may be lexicalized as single compounds (取りあう as 動詞/自立).
Our pattern focuses on the productive construction (split tokenization).
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Two-token matcher: VerbStem + AuDependentVerb
2. VerbStemMatcher: 動詞 in 連用形 (conjugative form)
3. AuDependentVerbMatcher: base_form == "あう" OR "ある", POS == 動詞/非自立
4. Handles base_form ambiguity: Kagome tokenizes あっ as either "あう" or "ある"
5. Pattern priority: 1 (low - construction pattern, detected but not highlighted)
6. Matches the productive suffix construction (verb stem + あう), not lexicalized compounds

**Pattern ranges**: Include verb stem + full conjugated form of あう
- Present: `話しあう` (chars 8-12) - verb stem + あう
- Past: `見せあった` (chars 11-16) - verb stem + あっ + た (full construction)
- Negative: `協力しあわない` (chars 18-25) - verb stem + あわ + ない (full construction)

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover major conjugation variants: dictionary, present, past, negative
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Tokenization Challenge Solved**:
The surface form あっ (連用タ接続) is ambiguous between:
- あう (to do together) → あっ
- ある (to exist) → あっ

Kagome may tokenize this as base_form="ある" even when it's the suffix あう. The matcher handles both base forms ("あう" OR "ある") when POS is 動詞/非自立, which correctly identifies the suffix usage.

**Related patterns**:
- に合わせて・に合った (N3) - "in accordance with" (different meaning of 合う)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: 全く～ない (not at all / completely not)

**JLPT Level**: N3

**Key implementation points**:

- Adjective: まったく + Adjective[く] + ない
- Noun: まったく + Noun + が + ない

- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: まったく is more formal and serious than 全然（ぜんぜん）, which has similar meaning but is more casual. まったく tends to appear in formal writing and serious situations, while 全然 is common in everyday casual speech. Both emphasize complete absence or total negation.

**Usage contexts**:
- Emphasis: Stressing the complete absence of something
- Formal contexts: More common in formal writing/speech than 全然
- Both forms: 全く (kanji) and まったく (hiragana) are equally acceptable

## Session 360 - 2025-12-14

### ✅ Session Summary
**Status**: COMPLETE - Successfully implemented 2 N4 patterns (の1, と2)
**Patterns completed**: 2 (の1 - nominalization, と2 - quotation particle)
**Tests added**: 10 new tests (5 for の1, 5 for と2 - all passing)
**Total tests**: 2305 passing, 1 ignored (was 2295 passing, 1 ignored - net +10 tests)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: の1 (nominalization - that which / the one who)

**JLPT Level**: N4
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low - not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: の1 is a nominalization particle that converts verb clauses into nouns, allowing them to function as subjects or objects in sentences. It's followed by は (topic marker) or が (subject marker). Translates to "that which", "the one who", or "the one that" in English.

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all major variants
1. Verb[る] + のは: 食べるのは (the one that eats) - chars 5-7
2. Verb[る] + のが: 乗るのが (that which is riding) - chars 7-9
3. Verb[た] + のは: 食べたのは (the one that ate) - chars 9-11
4. Verb[ている] + のは: 登っているのは (the one who is climbing) - chars 7-9
5. Verb[る] + のが: 走るのが (that which is running) - chars 4-6

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n4.rs (no1 function, lines 8124-8162)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n4_patterns.rs (no1_tests module, lines 11721-11790)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (No1 variant, lines 1061-1067)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
の as nominalizer (dependent noun):
- の (名詞/非自立/一般) - dependent noun
- Surface: の, Base: の
- POS: 名詞/非自立/一般

は or が following の:
- は (助詞/係助詞) - topic particle
- が (助詞/格助詞) - subject particle
- Surface: は or が
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Two-token matcher: の + (は|が)
2. NoNominalizerMatcher checks surface == "の", POS == 名詞/非自立/一般
3. WaGaMatcher matches BOTH は and が as particles
4. Pattern matches ONLY のは or のが (not the preceding verb clause)
5. Pattern priority: 1 (detected but not highlighted in overlay)
6. The verb clause before の can be any tense or form (dictionary, past, progressive, etc.)

**Pattern ranges**: Include only の + は/が (2 tokens)
- After verb[る]: のは or のが
- After verb[た]: のは
- After verb[ている]: のは
- Pattern captures just the nominalization marker, not the entire clause being nominalized

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json
- Cover all major structural variants: dictionary form, past, progressive, with は/が
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: の1 (nominalization) is distinct from the N5 の (pronoun replacement). The key difference:
- **N5 の**: Can stand alone as pronoun replacement (これは私の "this is mine")
- **N4 の1**: MUST be followed by は or が to form nominalization (食べるのは "the one that eats")

The nominalization allows entire verb clauses to function as noun phrases in the sentence. For example:
- 食べるのは彼です - "The one that eats is him" (literally: "As for eating, it's him")
- バスに乗るのが嫌い - "I hate riding the bus" (literally: "That which is riding a bus, [I] hate")

**Usage contexts**:
- Nominalizing verb clauses: Converting actions into noun phrases
- Topic/subject marking: Used with は or が to mark nominalized clauses as topics or subjects
- All registers: Works in both formal and informal contexts
- Cannot replace こと in set expressions: ことができる, ことがある (these require こと, not の)

**Caution from grammar data**:
- の cannot be used as substitute for こと in set expressions like ことができる or ことがある
- の may NOT be followed by だ, である, or です (this becomes explanatory のです/んです pattern)

**Related patterns**:
- の (N5) - pronoun replacement (already implemented)
- のです・んです (N4) - explanatory pattern (not yet implemented)
- こと (N4) - alternative nominalizer (not yet implemented)
- ことができる (N5) - "can do" (uses こと, not の)

---

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: と2 (quotation particle)

**JLPT Level**: N4
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low - not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: と2 is the quotation particle (引用/いんよう - citation/reference) used to mark quoted speech, thoughts, or reported information. It can follow almost any type of word or phrase, and is followed by quotation verbs like 言う (say), 思う (think), 聞く (hear), 考える (think/consider), etc. The verb is often omitted at sentence end.

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all major variants
1. Quoted phrase + と + 言う: 「３キロ歩いた」と言った (said "I walked 3km") - chars 13-14
2. Noun + だ + と + 思う: 猫だと思う (think it's a cat) - chars 5-6
3. な-Adjective + だ + と + 思う: 綺麗だと思う (think it's beautiful) - chars 7-8
4. Quoted phrase + と + 叫ぶ: 「危ない！」と叫んだ (yelled "Watch out!") - chars 9-10
5. Quoted phrase + と (verb omitted): 「何で？」と (asked "Why?") - chars 11-12

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n4.rs (to2 function, lines 8163-8185)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n4_patterns.rs (to2_tests module, lines 11790-11863)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (To2 variant, lines 1069-1075)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
と as quotation particle:
- と (助詞/格助詞/引用) - quotation/citation particle
- Surface: と, Base: と
- POS: 助詞/格助詞/引用 (particle/case particle/quotation)
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Single-token matcher: と (quotation particle only)
2. ToQuotationMatcher checks surface == "と", POS == 助詞/格助詞/引用
3. Pattern matches ONLY the quotation と (not the quoted content or following verb)
4. Pattern priority: 1 (detected but not highlighted in overlay)
5. The と must be specifically 助詞/格助詞/引用 (not other uses of と like conditional)
6. Quotation verbs (言う, 思う, 聞く, 考える, etc.) often follow but can be omitted

**Pattern ranges**: Include only と (1 token)
- After quoted phrase: と
- After noun + だ: と
- After adjective + だ: と
- Pattern captures just the quotation marker

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json
- Cover all major structural variants: quoted phrases, nouns, adjectives, verb omitted
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: と as a quotation particle expresses the relationship between (A) "what was communicated" and (B) "how it was communicated". It's one of the fundamental uses of と in Japanese (alongside the conditional と3 and compilation particle と). The quotation can be:
- Direct speech: 「危ない！」と叫んだ ("Watch out!" [he] yelled)
- Indirect thought: 猫だと思う (I think it's a cat)
- Reported information: 彼が来ると言った (He said that he'll come)

**Usage contexts**:
- Quotation/citation: Marks what was said, thought, or heard
- All registers: Works in both formal and informal contexts
- Verb omission: The quotation verb can be omitted at sentence end (conversational)
- Requires だ: After nouns and な-adjectives, だ is needed before と

**Caution from grammar data**:
- と requires だ when used after nouns or な-Adjectives
- The quotation verb will often be omitted at the end of a sentence
- と can appear after almost any type of word or phrase

**Related patterns**:
- と (N4) - conditional particle と3 (already implemented)
- と (N5) - compilation particle "and/with" (already implemented)
- とおもう (N4) - specific と思う pattern (already implemented)
- って (N3) - casual quotation marker (already implemented)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: から (from / because - dual usage particle)

**JLPT Level**: N5
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low - not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: から is a versatile particle with two main meanings:
1. **から1 (from)** - Marks starting point for location, time, or source (格助詞)
2. **から (because)** - Marks reason or cause, more subjective than ので (接続助詞)

The meaning is determined by the POS tag and context. The base nuance is always "from" - either "from a location" or "from a reason".

**Structures tested**: 7 comprehensive tests covering both usages

**から1 (from) - 3 tests (pre-existing)**:
1. Noun + から (location): モロッコから来ました (from Morocco) - chars 3-9
2. Noun + から (place): 空港から車で (from the airport) - chars 0-4
3. Noun + から (time): 今日から新しい仕事 (from today) - chars 0-4

**から (because) - 4 tests (newly added)**:
1. Verb + から: 食べたから (because I ate) - chars 15-18 (た + から)
2. い-Adjective + から: 優しいから (because she's kind) - chars 3-8
3. な-Adjective + だ + から: 便利だから (because it's convenient) - chars 4-9
4. Noun + だ + から: 会社だから (because it's a company) - chars 3-8

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n5.rs (kara function, lines 1657-1705)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n5_patterns.rs
  - kara_from_tests module (lines 346-378) - existing tests for から1
  - kara_because_tests module (lines 393-443) - new tests for から
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Kara variant, line 476)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
から as case particle (格助詞) - "from":
- Noun + から (starting point)
- Surface: から, Base: から
- POS: 助詞/格助詞

から as conjunction particle (接続助詞) - "because":
- Verb/Adjective/Auxiliary + から
- Surface: から, Base: から
- POS: 助詞/接続助詞
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Three-token matcher: (Verb/Adj/Noun/Aux) + Optional(だ) + から
2. VerbAdjNounAuxMatcher matches 動詞, 形容詞, 名詞, or 助動詞 (to catch た, etc.)
3. Optional(DaCopulaMatcher) handles だ after nouns/な-adjectives
4. KaraParticleMatcher accepts BOTH 格助詞 (from) AND 接続助詞 (because)
5. Pattern includes full construction (e.g., 便利だから not just だから)
6. Single pattern handles both usages based on POS tag context

**Pattern ranges**: Include full grammatical construction
- Verb + auxiliary + から: たから (after 食べた)
- Adjective + から: 優しいから
- Noun/な-adj + だ + から: 便利だから, 会社だから
- Location + から: モロッコから, 空港から
- Time + から: 今日から

**Test Quality**:
- All 7 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json
- Cover all major structural variants for both usages
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: The "because" usage (接続助詞) is more subjective and personal than ので, expressing the speaker's personal reasoning. The "from" usage (格助詞) marks literal starting points in space or time. Both share the base nuance of "originating from" - either from a location or from a reason/cause.

**Important distinction**:
- から (because) with nouns/な-adj REQUIRES だ: 学生だから
- から (from) with nouns does NOT use だ: 駅から (not 駅だから)

**Usage contexts**:
- Caution: から for "because" requires だ after nouns/な-adjectives
- The だ creates the explanatory/reasoning tone
- Without だ, から after a noun means "from that location"

**Related patterns**:
- ので (N5) - "because/since" (more objective/formal than から)
- てから (N5) - "after doing" (different usage of から)
- から1 (N5) - "from" (same pattern, just the location/time meaning)
- だから (N5) - "therefore/so" (standalone usage at sentence start)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: と (and / with - compilation particle)

**JLPT Level**: N5
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low - not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: と is a particle that shows a relationship of 'compilation' between (A) and (B). That relationship could be existing together (translated as "and"), or performing an action together (translated as "with"). This is the basic N5 と particle (distinguished from the N4 quotation と and conditional と).

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all major usage contexts
1. Noun + と (and): ピーナッツとビール (peanuts and beer) - chars 0-6
2. Noun + と (with - companion): 彼女と食べる (eat with girlfriend) - chars 0-3
3. Multiple と in sequence: 消しゴムとペンと鉛筆 (eraser, pen, and pencil) - chars 0-5 (first と)
4. Pronoun + と: 私と君 (me and you) - chars 0-2
5. Natural dialogue: 友達と映画 (with friend to movie) - chars 3-6

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n5.rs (to function, lines 111-130)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n5_patterns.rs (to_tests module, lines 6678-6746)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (ToAnd variant, declared at lines 75-81)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
と as coordinate particle (並立助詞):
- Used when listing items: Noun + と + Noun
- Surface: と, Base: と
- POS: 助詞/並立助詞

と as case particle (格助詞):
- Used for "with" (companion):  Noun + と + Verb
- Surface: と, Base: と
- POS: 助詞/格助詞/一般
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Two-token matcher: Noun + と
2. ToParticleMatcher matches BOTH 並立助詞 (coordinate) AND 格助詞 (case) variants
3. Uses `noun_matcher()` to match preceding noun (including pronouns)
4. Pattern includes the full Noun + と construction
5. Named "ToAnd" variant to distinguish from existing "To" (N4 conditional と3)
6. Multiple と in a sentence are all detected separately

**Pattern ranges**: Include noun + と particle
- ピーナッツと (chars 0-6)
- 彼女と (chars 0-3)
- 消しゴムと (chars 0-5), ペンと (chars 5-8)
- 私と (chars 0-2)
- 友達と (chars 3-6)

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json
- Cover all major structural variants: and (listing), with (companion), multiple と, pronouns
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: と is one of the fundamental Japanese particles. As a compilation particle, it shows items or people grouped together. The "and" translation is used when listing things (A と B と C), while the "with" translation is used when showing companionship in actions (A と B を食べる = eat A with B, or A と食べる = eat with A). The last と in a sequence is often omitted in casual conversation.

**Usage contexts**:
- Listing items: Connecting multiple nouns
- Companionship: Performing actions together with someone
- Both formal and informal registers
- Required after each noun except optionally the last

**Related patterns**:
- と1 (N5) - (not yet implemented)
- と2 (N4) - quotation particle (not yet implemented)
- と3 / To (N4) - conditional particle (already implemented)
- や (N4) - "and/or" (similar but less exhaustive listing)

**Pattern naming note**:
- This pattern is named "ToAnd" as the enum variant to avoid collision with existing "To" variant
- The existing "To" variant (line 1053) is for the N4 conditional と3
- Feature_list.json tracks this as "と" (N5) pattern name
- Multiple と patterns exist: と (N5 compilation), と1 (N5), と2 (N4 quotation), と3 (N4 conditional)

### ✅ Pattern Implemented: 最中に (right in the middle of / in the midst of)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 3

**Pattern**: 最中（さいちゅう）に strengthens the meaning of 中（ちゅう）に to show that something is 'right in the middle of happening' or 'in the midst of (A)'. As the kanji 最（さい） means 'utmost', the literal meaning is 'utmost middle'. Can be translated as 'at the height of (A)' or 'in the midst of (A)'. Usually followed by (B) that interrupts or disturbs (A).

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all major variants
1. Verb[ている] + 最中に: 食べている最中に (right in the middle of eating) - chars 8-11
2. Noun + の + 最中に: 仕事の最中に (in the middle of work) - chars 3-6
3. Verb[ている] + 最中だ: 追いかけている最中だ (in the midst of chasing) - chars 12-15
4. Verb[ている] + 最中です: している最中です (in the middle of doing - polite) - chars 11-15
5. Natural subtitle: 見ている最中に (in the middle of watching) - chars 7-10

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (saichuuni function, lines 1890-1944)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (saichuuni_tests module, lines 12418-12478)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Saichuuni pattern, declared at line 2905)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
最中に variant:
- 最中 (名詞/副詞可能) - noun with adverbial usage "utmost middle"
- に (助詞/格助詞/一般) - case particle

最中だ variant:
- 最中 (名詞/副詞可能) - noun with adverbial usage
- だ (助動詞, base=だ, 特殊・ダ, 基本形) - copula auxiliary

最中です variant:
- 最中 (名詞/副詞可能) - noun with adverbial usage
- です (助動詞, base=です, 特殊・デス, 基本形) - polite copula
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Two-token matcher: 最中 + (に|だ|です)
2. SaichuuMatcher checks surface == "最中", base_form == "最中", POS == 名詞/副詞可能
3. NiDaDesuMatcher matches に (case particle), だ (auxiliary), OR です (auxiliary)
4. Pattern matches just 最中に/だ/です (2 tokens), not the preceding verb/noun
5. Pattern priority: 3 (moderate - detected but not highlighted in browser overlay)
6. Usually used with Verb[ている] form or Noun + の

**Pattern ranges**: Include only 最中 + ending particle/auxiliary (2 tokens)
- After verb[ている]: 最中に
- After noun + の: 最中に
- With copula: 最中だ
- With polite copula: 最中です
- Pattern captures just the "in the midst of" marker

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover all major structural variants: ている+最中に, noun+の+最中に, 最中だ, 最中です
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: 最中（さいちゅう） emphasizes being at the pinnacle or height of an action/event. Usually followed by something that interrupts or disturbs what's happening. More emphatic than simple 中（ちゅう）に. The pattern implies the action is ongoing and something happened during it.

**Usage contexts**:
- Interruptions: Something happens while in the midst of an action
- Emphasis: Stressing the timing of an event at the peak of something
- Formal/informal: Works in both registers (最中に/最中だ casual, 最中です polite)

**Related patterns**:
- 中（ちゅう）に (N4) - "during/while" (less emphatic version)
- ている (N5) - progressive/resultative form (often precedes 最中)
- 間に (N4) - "while/during" (similar but different nuance)

### ✅ Pattern Completed: 上で (upon / after - formal progression)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 3

**Pattern**: 上（うえ）で is a formal expression that shows logical progression from action (A) to action (B). It combines the noun 上 ("above/upon") with the case marking particle で. The pattern translates to "upon (A)" or "after (A)", strongly implying that (A) is a requirement or expected step before (B). It's primarily used in formal settings such as business, legal documents, and instructions.

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering both major variants
1. Verb[た] + 上で: 相談した上で (upon consulting with the CEO) - chars 12-14
2. Verb[た] + 上で: 運転した上で (upon driving various cars) - chars 9-11
3. Noun + の + 上で: 確認の上で (upon confirming the details) - chars 9-11
4. Noun + の + 上で: ログインの上で (upon logging in) - chars 13-15

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (uede function, lines 1896-1928)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (uede_tests module, lines 12356-12403)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Uede pattern)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
Verb[た] + 上で:
- た (助動詞, base=た, 特殊・タ, 基本形) - past tense auxiliary
- 上 (名詞/非自立/副詞可能) - dependent noun "above/upon"
- で (助詞/格助詞/一般) - case particle

Noun + の + 上で:
- の (助詞/連体化) - nominalizing particle
- 上 (名詞/非自立/副詞可能) - dependent noun "above/upon"
- で (助詞/格助詞/一般) - case particle
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Two-token matcher: 上 + で
2. UeMatcher checks surface == "上", base_form == "上", POS == 名詞/非自立
3. DeParticleMatcher matches で as particle (助詞/格助詞), NOT auxiliary verb で from だ
4. Pattern matches just 上で (doesn't include preceding verb or noun)
5. Pattern priority: 3 (moderate - detected and highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern ranges**: Include only 上で (2 tokens)
- After verb[た]: 上で
- After noun + の: 上で
- Pattern captures just the formal progression marker, not the prerequisite action

**Test Quality**:
- All 4 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover both major structural variants: verb[た] form, noun + の form
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: The grammar data emphasizes that 上で strongly hints at (A) being a requirement for (B), or (A) being something logically expected before (B). This is different from simple temporal succession - it implies necessary steps in a process. For example, "upon consulting with a doctor" (before using medication) or "upon confirming the details" (before signing).

**Important Caution**: Due to 上で showing logical progressions, (A) cannot be a passive verb. Passivity in Japanese implies lack of control, which would make (B) sound unplanned. For example:
- ❌ 医者に教えられた上で、使用してください (unnatural - "upon being told by a doctor")
- ✅ 医者に相談した上で、使用してください (natural - "upon consulting with a doctor")

**Usage contexts**:
- Formal instructions: Procedural steps in documentation
- Business communication: Professional emails and memos
- Legal/official documents: Terms and conditions, agreements
- Requirements: Actions that must be completed before others

**Related patterns**:
- た後で (N4) - "after doing" (less formal, simple temporal succession)
- てから (N4) - "after doing" (casual temporal succession)
- 上 (N3) - standalone "above/on top of" (spatial/abstract)

### ✅ Pattern Completed: 一方で (on the other hand / while / at the same time)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority, not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: 一方で combines the noun 一方（いっぽう）'one direction' with the case marking particle で to create a conjunction particle meaning "on the other hand", "while", or "at the same time". It's used to contrast or compare two situations that exist simultaneously. The pattern can follow verbs/adjectives in their attributive form, or appear at the beginning of a sentence. The で particle is optional in some contexts.

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all major variants
1. Verb + 一方で: 晴れる一方で (while it will be sunny in Kansai) - chars 9-12
2. Verb + 一方 (without で): いる一方 (while there are affectionate cats) - chars 12-14
3. な-Adjective + な + 一方で: 静かな一方で (while it's quiet during the day) - chars 10-13
4. Noun + である + 一方で: 歌手である一方で (he is a singer, while at the same time) - chars 7-10
5. Sentence-initial: 一方で (on the other hand) - chars 0-3

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (ippoude function, lines 7657-7689)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (ippoude_tests module, lines 12294-12354)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Ippoude, declared at line 4065)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
After verbs/adjectives (一方 as noun):
- 一方 (名詞/非自立/副詞可能) - dependent noun
- で (助詞/格助詞/一般) - case particle (optional)

Sentence-initial (一方 as conjunction):
- 一方 (接続詞) - conjunction
- で (助詞/格助詞/一般) - case particle (optional)
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Two-token matcher: 一方 + Optional(で)
2. IppouMatcher matches 一方 as BOTH noun (名詞/非自立/副詞可能) AND conjunction (接続詞)
3. DeParticleMatcher matches で as particle (助詞/格助詞), NOT auxiliary verb で from だ
4. Optional(で) allows the pattern to work with or without the で particle
5. Pattern priority: 1 (detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern ranges**: Include 一方 with optional で
- With で: 一方で (2 tokens)
- Without で: 一方 (1 token)
- Pattern captures just the conjunction itself, not the preceding clause

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover all major structural variants: verb, na-adjective, noun+である, sentence-initial, with/without で
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: The grammar data emphasizes the contrast between 一方で (conjunction particle, "at one direction/side") and 一方だ (auxiliary verb pattern, "in one direction"). The で in 一方で is a case particle showing location/context, whereas the で in 一方で is the conjunctive form of the copula だ. Context usually makes it clear which pattern is being used:
- 一方で expresses comparison/contrast between two situations
- 一方だ expresses continuous change in a single direction

**Usage contexts**:
- Contrasting situations: Showing two opposing or different situations that coexist
- Simultaneous situations: Events or states happening at the same time
- Sentence continuation: Starting a new sentence with contrasting information
- Formal writing: Common in news, reports, and formal explanations

**Caution**: Don't confuse 一方で with 一方だ (implemented in previous session):
- 一方で + Phrase: "While/on the other hand" - shows contrast between situations
- Verb + 一方だ: "keeps (verb)ing" - shows continuous change in one direction

**Related patterns**:
- 一方だ (N3) - continuous change in one direction (already implemented)
- 一方 (N3) - standalone conjunction meaning "while/whereas" (same pattern without で)
- その一方で - "on the other hand" with その for emphasis

### ✅ Pattern Completed: んだって (I heard that / it's thought that)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority, not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: んだって is a casual sentence-ending particle that expresses something heard by the speaker. It combines ん (explanatory particle) with だって (a contraction of だ + って). The pattern translates to "I heard that (A)" or "it's thought that (A)". だって is a grouping of the auxiliary verb だ and the adverbial particle って (abbreviation of と言って 'is said' or と思って 'is thought').

**Structures tested**: 4 comprehensive tests covering all major variants
1. Verb + んだって: するんだって (I heard she's getting married) - chars 12-16
2. い-Adjective + んだって: 欲しいんだって (I heard he wants it) - chars 16-20
3. な-Adjective + な + んだって: 適当なんだって (I heard it's all nonsense) - chars 37-41
4. Noun + な + んだって: フィギュアスケートなんだって (I heard his favorite sport is figure skating) - chars 40-44

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (ndatte function, lines 3465-3541)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (ndatte_tests module, lines 12197-12250)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Ndatte, declared at line 3264)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
Two different tokenization patterns depending on preceding word:

Pattern 1 (after verbs):
- ん (助動詞, 不変化型) - explanatory auxiliary
- だって (助詞/終助詞) - sentence-ending particle (single token)

Pattern 2 (after adjectives/nouns):
- ん (名詞/非自立/一般) - explanatory dependent noun
- だ (助動詞, base=だ) - copula auxiliary
- って (助詞/格助詞/連語) - quotative particle
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Three-token matcher: ん + Optional(だ) + (だって|って)
2. NParticleMatcher matches ん as BOTH 助動詞 (after verbs) AND 名詞/非自立 (after adj/nouns)
3. DaAuxMatcher matches だ as auxiliary verb (助動詞, base=だ)
4. DatteOrTteMatcher matches EITHER:
   - だって (助詞/終助詞) - after verbs
   - って (助詞/格助詞/連語) - after adjectives/nouns
5. Optional(だ) handles both tokenization patterns in a single matcher
6. Pattern priority: 1 (detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern ranges**: Include only んだって (or ん + だ + って)
- After verbs: んだって (2 tokens: ん + だって)
- After adjectives/nouns: んだって (3 tokens: ん + だ + って)
- The pattern is a sentence-ending particle, so it doesn't include the preceding content word

**Test Quality**:
- All 4 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover all structural variants: verb, i-adjective, na-adjective, noun
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: なんだって is quite casual and should be avoided in formal situations. It's commonly used in conversations with friends to relay information heard from others. The pattern expresses hearsay or secondhand information, similar to "I heard that..." in English.

**Usage contexts**:
- Casual conversation: Relaying information heard from others
- Informal situations: Friends, family, casual social contexts
- Expresses hearsay: Information not directly witnessed by the speaker

**Related patterns**:
- ～んです・のです (N4) - explanatory ん pattern (different meaning)
- って (N4) - quotative particle (component of だって)
- だ (N5) - copula (component of だって)

### ✅ Pattern Completed: 一方だ (more and more / continuing to / getting X-er and X-er)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority, not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: 一方（いっぽう） is a noun meaning "single direction" that follows verbs expressing change to convey continuing progression in one direction. Translates to "more and more (A)", "continuing to (A)", or "getting (A)er and (A)er". The pattern emphasizes that the change is unidirectional and ongoing. 一方 is paired with だ (sentence-ending) or で (mid-sentence with explanation).

**Structures tested**: 3 comprehensive tests covering major variants
1. Verb + 一方だ: 悪化する一方だ (keeps getting worse) - chars 16-19
2. Verb + 一方です (polite): 減る一方です (population keeps decreasing) - chars 13-17
3. Verb + 一方だ (increase): 上がる一方だ (keeps rising) - chars 9-12

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (ippouda function, lines 7620-7654)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (ippouda_tests module, lines 12252-12292)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Ippouda, declared at line 4058)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
Standard form:
- 一方 (名詞/非自立/副詞可能) - dependent noun
- だ (助動詞, base=だ, 基本形) - copula auxiliary

Polite form:
- 一方 (名詞/非自立/副詞可能) - dependent noun
- です (助動詞, base=です, 基本形) - polite copula auxiliary
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Two-token matcher: 一方 + (だ|です)
2. IppouMatcher checks surface == "一方", base_form == "一方", POS == 名詞/非自立/副詞可能
3. DaDesuMatcher matches BOTH だ (base=だ) and です (base=です) as auxiliary verbs
4. Pattern priority: 1 (detected but not highlighted in overlay)
5. Only used with verbs expressing change (increase, decrease, intensify, weaken)
6. Literal meaning "single direction" conveys continuing change in one direction

**Pattern ranges**: Include 一方だ or 一方です (2 tokens)
- Plain form: 一方だ (chars 16-19)
- Polite form: 一方です (chars 13-17)
- Pattern captures just 一方 + copula, not the preceding verb

**Test Quality**:
- All 3 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover both plain (だ) and polite (です) forms
- Test both increasing and decreasing verbs
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: 一方 can ONLY be used with verbs that express some kind of change, as 一方 itself does not express change - it only indicates a continuing direction. Works with verbs expressing increasing, decreasing, intensifying, or weakening, so long as the shift is all in a single direction. The pattern emphasizes the unidirectional, ongoing nature of the change.

**Usage examples from grammar data**:
- 悪化する一方だ: "The relationship keeps getting worse" (deterioration)
- 減る一方です: "The population keeps decreasing" (decline)
- 上がる一方だ: "Prices keep rising" (increase)
- 増える一方で: "Because the amount of work keeps increasing..." (with explanation)

**Caution**: Unnatural uses occur when 一方 is paired with verbs that don't express change:
- 泣く一方だ: "Cries in one direction" (unnatural - 泣く doesn't express change)
- 進む一方だ: "Keeps progressing" (natural - 進む expresses change)

**Related patterns**:
- 一方で (N3) - same pattern, mid-sentence form with で (not yet implemented)
- ばかり (N4) - "nothing but / only" (different meaning but similar continual sense)

The additional expressions として (or とする) are sometimes paired with it to further emphasize (A), similar to "with (A) as the starting point, (B)". The pattern can be used with multiple items listed after the starting point.

### ✅ Pattern Completed: んじゃない (don't do / prohibition)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority, not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: んじゃない is a slightly slang grammar pattern used with verbs to prohibit or request that something not be done. It is usually translated as "don't do (A)" or "you mustn't (A)". んじゃない is almost always used from a higher position toward someone of a lower status. It may be used when the speaker is angry, but not always.

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all major variants
1. Verb + んじゃない (basic prohibition): 言うんじゃない (don't say) - chars 11-18
2. Verb + んじゃない (with exclamation): 食べるんじゃない (don't eat) - chars 9-17
3. Verb + んじゃありません (polite): 取るんじゃありません (you shouldn't take) - chars 10-20
4. Verb + てん + じゃない (てる abbreviation): てんじゃない (don't be doing) - chars 15-21
5. Verb + んじゃなかった (past regret): 買うんじゃなかった (shouldn't have bought) - chars 7-16

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (njanai function, lines 4066-4128)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (njanai_tests module, lines 12129-12195)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Njanai, declared at line 3393)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
Basic structure: Verb + ん + じゃ + ない/ありません/なかった
てん variant: Verb + て + ん + じゃ + ない

Verb (required):
- 動詞 in dictionary form (基本形) - for basic prohibition
- て (助詞/接続助詞) - for てん contraction

ん (required):
- ん (名詞/非自立/一般) - explanatory particle

じゃ (required):
- じゃ (助詞/副助詞) - contraction of では

Negative ending (required):
- ない (助動詞, 特殊・ナイ, 基本形) - plain negative
- あり (助動詞, base=ある, 連用形) - start of polite ありません
- なかっ (助動詞, base=ない) - start of past なかった
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Four-token matcher: Verb/て + ん + じゃ + ない/あり/なかっ
2. VerbOrTeMatcher matches BOTH dictionary form verbs AND て particle
3. Handles て + ん contraction (abbreviated ている → てん)
4. NParticleMatcher checks surface == "ん", POS == 名詞/非自立
5. JyaMatcher checks surface == "じゃ", POS == 助詞
6. NegativeFormMatcher handles three endings: ない, あり (from ありません), なかっ (from なかった)
7. Pattern matcher automatically extends あり to include the full ありません
8. Pattern priority: 1 (detected but not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern ranges**: Include verb/て + complete negative construction
- Basic: 言うんじゃない (chars 11-18) - verb + ん + じゃ + ない
- Polite: 取るんじゃありません (chars 10-20) - auto-extends to full ません + ん
- てん form: てんじゃない (chars 15-21) - て + ん + じゃ + ない
- Past: 買うんじゃなかった (chars 7-16) - verb + ん + じゃ + なかっ + た

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover all major structural variants: basic, polite, てん abbreviation, past regret
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: んじゃない is a combination of の (case-marking particle) and the abbreviated form of ではない (じゃない). It connects to the dictionary form of verbs. The more literal translation is "it's not (verb)", similar to "you're not (verb)" where a person is directly telling another what they shouldn't do. The てん variant is common in casual speech, contracting ている to てん before じゃない.

**Usage contexts**:
- Higher to lower status: Parent to child, teacher to student, boss to subordinate
- May express anger but not always: Can also be gentle correction
- Polite form (んじゃありません): Less angry, used when adult talks to child
- Past form (んじゃなかった): Regret or disappointment that action occurred

**Caution**: When んじゃない comes at sentence end with rising intonation, it may be a question (abbreviation of んじゃないか), not a prohibition. The difference is clear from intonation: questions have rising tone, orders have dropping tone.

**Related patterns**:
- じゃない (N5) - generic negative "is not" (broader pattern)
- のではない (N3) - explanatory negative (more formal)
- ないで (N4) - "without doing" or "don't do" (different construction)

### ✅ Pattern Completed: わけがない (there's no way that / it's impossible that / SO not)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority, not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: わけがない is one of the strongest ways to use the noun わけ in Japanese. It combines わけ (reason/conclusion) with が (case particle) and ない (negative adjective) to express impossibility or strong negation. It is regularly translated as "there is no way that (A)", or "it is impossible that (A)". The pattern emphasizes the non-existence of something and is much stronger than わけではない. Can be thought of as "it is SO not (A)" (for わけがない) vs "it's not that (A)" (for わけではない).

**Structures tested**: 6 comprehensive tests covering all major variants
1. Verb[る] + わけがない: 食べれるわけがない (there's no way I can eat) - chars 26-31
2. Verb[た] + わけがない: 持ってたわけがない (there's no way he had) - chars 19-24
3. い-Adjective + わけがない: 不味いわけがない (there's no way it's bad) - chars 19-27 (extended to include じゃん)
4. な-Adjective + な + わけがない: 静かなわけがない (there's no way it's quiet) - chars 19-24
5. Verb + わけがありません (polite): 暇なわけがありません (there's no way he has free time) - chars 18-26
6. い-Adjective + わけがありません (polite): 意地悪なわけがありません (there's no way it's mean) - chars 15-23

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (wakeganai function, lines 4071-4119)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (wakeganai_tests module, lines 11898-11992)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Wakeganai, declared at line 3401)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
Three-token matcher: わけ + が + ない/あり

わけ (required):
- わけ (名詞/非自立/一般) - dependent noun

が (required):
- が (助詞/格助詞/一般) - case particle

ない or あり (required):
- ない (形容詞/自立, base=ない, 基本形) - plain negative
- あり (動詞/自立, base=ある, 連用形) - start of polite negative (ありません)
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Three-token matcher: わけ + が + ない/あり
2. WakeMatcher checks surface == "わけ", POS == 名詞/非自立
3. GaMatcher checks surface == "が", POS == 助詞/格助詞
4. NaiAriMatcher matches EITHER ない (adjective) OR あり (verb, start of ありません)
5. Pattern matcher automatically extends あり to include the full ありません
6. The pattern matches just わけがない or わけがありません
7. Pattern priority: 1 (detected but not highlighted in overlay)
8. Auto-extension: Since ない is an adjective, the pattern gets extended to include following auxiliary verbs (e.g., じゃん)

**Pattern ranges**: Include わけがない or わけがありません
- Plain form: わけがない (3 tokens)
- Polite form: わけがありません (わけ + が + あり, then auto-extended to ありません)
- Note: Patterns containing adjectives are auto-extended to include auxiliary verbs

**Test Quality**:
- All 6 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover all major structural variants: verb (る/た), i-adj, na-adj, polite forms
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants (including auto-extension)

**Linguistic Note**: わけがない is much stronger than わけではない. It emphasizes the complete impossibility or non-existence of something, similar to "it is SO not (A)" in English. The difference can be understood as:
- わけではない: "it is so that it isn't (A)" - highlights that (A) exists but not in the way stated
- わけがない: "it is SO not (A)" - highlights that no part of (A) can be considered correct/accurate

**Related patterns**:
- わけだ (N3) - "so / no wonder / that's why" (affirmative logical conclusion)
- わけではない (N3) - "it's not that" (softer negation)
- わけにはいかない (N3) - "cannot" (obligation/impossibility)

### ✅ Pattern Completed: わけにはいかない (cannot afford to / impossible to / it cannot be so that)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 3

**Pattern**: わけにはいかない combines わけ (reason/conclusion) with に (case particle), は (topic/contrast particle), and いかない (negative form of いく "to go"). It expresses that something is highly undesirable or highly unattainable. The literal meaning is "it cannot be so that (A)" or "it cannot go in the direction of (A)". It conveys a stronger sense of impossibility or inappropriateness compared to simple できない.

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering different usage contexts
1. Verb + わけにはいかない (undesirable action): 残すわけにはいかない (there's no way I can leave it) - chars 30-38
2. Verb + わけにはいかない (cannot afford to): 休むわけにはいかない (cannot afford to take a day off) - chars 15-23
3. Verb + わけにはいかない (desirable but impossible): 買うわけにはいかない (it cannot be so that I buy it) - chars 29-37
4. Verb + わけにはいかない (social obligation): 断るわけにはいかない (there's no way I can decline) - chars 12-20
5. Verb + わけにはいきません (polite): 帰るわけにはいきません (there's no way I can go home) - chars 18-27

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (wakenihaikanai function, lines 5571-5643)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (wakenihaikanai_tests module, lines 11994-12072)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Wakenihaikanai)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
Five-token matcher: わけ + に + は + いか/いき + ない/ませ

わけ (required):
- わけ (名詞/非自立/一般) - dependent noun

に (required):
- に (助詞/格助詞/一般) - case particle

は (required):
- は (助詞/係助詞) - topic/contrast particle

いか or いき (required):
- いか (動詞/自立, base=いく, 未然形) - negative stem for plain form
- いき (動詞/自立, base=いく, 連用形) - connective stem for polite form

ない or ませ (required):
- ない (助動詞, base=ない, 基本形) - plain negative
- ませ (助動詞, base=ます, 未然形) - start of polite negative (ません)
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Five-token matcher: わけ + に + は + いか/いき + ない/ませ
2. WakeMatcher checks surface == "わけ", POS == 名詞/非自立
3. NiMatcher checks surface == "に", POS == 助詞/格助詞
4. WaMatcher checks surface == "は", POS == 助詞/係助詞
5. IkaMatcher matches EITHER いか (未然形) OR いき (連用形) from いく verb
6. NaiMaseMatcher matches EITHER ない (auxiliary) OR ませ (start of ません)
7. Pattern matcher automatically extends ませ to include the full ません
8. The pattern matches just わけにはいかない or わけにはいきません
9. Pattern priority: 3 (moderate - detected and highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern ranges**: Include full わけにはいかない or わけにはいきません
- Plain form: わけにはいかない (5 tokens: わけ + に + は + いか + ない)
- Polite form: わけにはいきません (わけ + に + は + いき + ませ, then auto-extended to ません + ん)
- Pattern extension handles both plain and polite negative endings

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover different usage contexts: undesirable action, cannot afford, impossible, social obligation, polite form
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: わけにはいかない expresses that something is highly inappropriate, undesirable, or impossible due to circumstances, social obligations, or moral reasons. It's stronger than simple できない (cannot) and carries connotations of "must not" or "should not". The pattern can express:
1. Undesirable actions that one feels compelled to avoid
2. Things one cannot afford to do given the circumstances
3. Desirable things that are impossible due to external constraints
4. Social/moral obligations that prevent certain actions

**Usage contexts**:
- Undesirable: Actions one morally/socially should not do
- Cannot afford: Actions impossible due to circumstances
- Impossible: Desired actions blocked by constraints
- Obligation: Social expectations preventing refusal

**Related patterns**:
- わけだ (N3) - "so / no wonder / that's why" (logical conclusion)
- わけではない (N3) - "it's not that" (softer negation)
- わけがない (N3) - "there's no way that" (impossibility/strong negation)
- できない (N5) - "cannot" (simple inability)

### ✅ Pattern Completed: わけだ (so/no wonder/that's why - logical conclusion)

**JLPT Level**: N3
**Category**: Construction
**Priority**: 1 (low priority, not highlighted in overlay)

**Pattern**: わけだ is one of the most misunderstood words in Japanese. わけ (訳) means "reason", "cause", "meaning", or "conclusion", but functions similarly to the English word "so". The pattern わけだ highlights the plain existence/determination of something, often translated as "for the reason of (A)", "no wonder it's (A)", or "it's (A), as you'd expect". It simply means "it is so that it's (A)" or "so it's (A)".

**Structures tested**: 5 comprehensive tests covering all major variants
1. Verb + という + わけだ: だから珍しく遅れて来たというわけだ (So that's why you came in late) - chars 11-17
2. い-Adjective + わけだ: どうりで一年たっても下手なわけだ (No wonder you haven't improved) - chars 13-16
3. な-Adjective + な + わけだ: だから定休日なわけだ (So that's why they are closed) - chars 7-10
4. Noun + の + わけだ: あなたは学生のわけだね (So you're a student, right?) - chars 7-10
5. Verb + という + わけです (polite): だから指を動かしたら痛かったというわけですね (So that's why it hurt) - chars 14-21

**Implementation details**:
- Matcher: grammar-lib/src/matchers/n3.rs (wakeda, lines 3259-3299)
- Tests: grammar-lib/src/tests/n3_patterns.rs (wakeda_tests module, lines 11733-11808)
- Pattern: grammar-lib/src/patterns.rs (Wakeda, declared at line 3224)

**Tokenization patterns**:
```
Three-token matcher: Optional(という) + わけ + だ/です

という (optional):
- という (助詞/格助詞/連語) - quotation particle

わけ (required):
- わけ (名詞/非自立/一般) - dependent noun

だ/です (required):
- だ (助動詞, base=だ, 基本形)
- です (助動詞, base=です, 基本形)
```

**Key implementation points**:
1. Three-token matcher with optional という: Optional(という) + わけ + だ/です
2. ToiuMatcher checks surface == "という", POS == 助詞/格助詞
3. WakeMatcher checks surface == "わけ", POS == 名詞/非自立
4. DaDesuMatcher checks base_form == "だ" or "です", POS == 助動詞
5. The pattern matches just わけだ/わけです (or というわけだ/というわけです), NOT including preceding の or な
6. わけ is a noun that follows the attributive form of the preceding word:
   - Verbs/い-Adjectives → already in attributive form
   - な-Adjectives + な → attributive marker
   - Nouns + の → attributive marker
7. Priority set to 1 (low) as per patterns.rs

**Pattern ranges**: Include わけだ/わけです, optionally with という
- Verb + という: "というわけだ" (chars 11-17)
- い-Adj: "わけだ" (chars 13-16) - な before わけ is the な-adj marker for 下手
- な-Adj: "わけだ" (chars 7-10)
- Noun: "わけだ" (chars 7-10)
- Polite: "というわけです" (chars 14-21)

**Test Quality**:
- All 5 tests include assert_has_pattern and assert_pattern_range
- No print_debug in final committed tests
- Realistic subtitle-quality sentences from grammar_points_data.json examples
- Cover all major structural variants: verb, i-adj, na-adj, noun, polite
- Correct character ranges verified for all variants

**Linguistic Note**: The grammar data explains that わけ should be considered almost identical to the English word "so" in its versatility. Due to わけ being a noun, it needs to follow the attributive form of whatever word comes before it. The pattern may also be used in questions (わけだね? / わけですか?) implying the speaker is trying to determine something they have heard.

**Related patterns**:
- わけではない (N3) - "it's not that" (negative form)
- わけがない (N3) - "there's no way that" (impossibility)
- わけにはいかない (N3) - "cannot" (obligation/impossibility)
